# Lane claims

Coordination ledger for parallel agents working this repo. Purpose: avoid the
duplicated-effort collisions that occurred on the Sylvester analytic lane
(2026-07-23), where two agents independently proved the same five theorems.

**Identity note (2026-07-23):** agents are identified by their *human*, not by
the model running them (models change mid-session). Rows previously labeled
`opus` were jon's agent; rows labeled `fable` were edward's agent. Edward's
agent stopped after the circle-Riesz definitional layer; jon's agent (also
running Fable 5) inherited and completed that lane. Edward's agent has since
RESUMED (2026-07-23) and claims lanes under `edward (resumed)` below.

**Completed lanes live in [`LANES-COMPLETED.md`](LANES-COMPLETED.md).** On
2026-07-29 this file was 661 KB across 213 rows, 170 of them finished; those
moved to the archive verbatim and 43 live rows remain here. Read the archive for
the reasoning behind completed work — several of its rows are the only written
account of why an approach does not work — but **claim only from this file**.

## Rules

**A lane is in exactly one of three states, and its row is the only thing that
says which.** Get this right and the rest of the file is detail.

- **open** — there is work to do and **nobody is doing it**. Row is in this file
  with no owner; `python3 scripts/lane.py free` offers it.
- **claimed** — an agent is working it **right now**. Row is in this file.
- **completed** — the work is finished. Row moves to
  [`LANES-COMPLETED.md`](LANES-COMPLETED.md).

`claimed` means *actively working*, not *intending to*, not *worked on it
yesterday*, not *owner of record*. **If you stop, it goes back to open.**

### The lane loop, start to finish

```text
1. lane.py free                 pick one; nothing else tells you what is takeable
2. lane.py check <LANE>         refuses if anyone holds it, on any branch
3. write the row, commit, PUSH  ← before the first edit, not with the first result
4. lane.py check <LANE> again   the push is your serialization point
5. do the work
6. build + gates, by exit code  never by piping into grep/tail -- see below
7. DONE first in the status cell, commit, PUSH
8. move the row to LANES-COMPLETED.md, PUSH
9. fetch + merge the other remotes, PUSH
```

Steps 3, 7, 8 and 9 are each a **push**. A row that is committed but not pushed
is a note to yourself; work that is finished but not pushed is invisible; a
finished row left on the live board is read and skipped by everyone after you.

The two steps agents actually skip are **4** and **7–8**. Skipping 4 cost a
duplicated lane on 2026-07-30 by 92 seconds. Skipping 7–8 left five *finished*
lanes printed under `HELD — do not take`, blocking other agents on work that was
already done — see *Three ways the board lies* and *Closing a lane is a push, not
a commit* below.

### The rest of the rules

- A claim covers the **listed declarations**, not the whole file. Two agents may
  work different declarations in one file if both are listed.
- **Unlisted = unclaimed.** If you want something not listed, add a row first.
- If two claims would overlap, **the earlier-committed row wins**; the other
  agent picks something else. Compare with
  `git log -1 --format=%cI -S '<row text>' <branch> -- dev/LANES.md`.
- **Release explicitly when you stop**, even mid-lane: `released — not started,
  taking X instead` is a complete and useful row. Silence reads as ownership.

### When these rules apply — and when they do not

**This file governs autonomous lane work.** It exists because agents picking
their own next task from a shared backlog collide; every rule here is a
consequence of that.

**If your human gave you a specific task, you are not required to claim a row.**
Being told "fix the docstrings in `Foo.lean`" or "finish this proof" is a
direct instruction, not a lane, and stopping to negotiate a claim for it is
overhead with no collision to prevent. The rules bind when you are *choosing*
work — taking a posted lane, picking from the backlog, or starting a sweep
nobody asked for.

Two things still apply either way, because they protect *other* agents rather
than coordinating you:

- If you discover mid-task that a live row already names your files, say so and
  coordinate. A direct instruction does not entitle you to overwrite work in
  progress.
- If a direct task grows into a multi-file campaign, it has become a lane in
  everything but name. Claim it at that point.

When in doubt, claiming is cheap and a collision is not. One row costs a minute;
the D-DOC collision cost two agents a full duplicated lane.

### Lane ids, prerequisites, and slicing

Two markers in a row's **status cell** make the board machine-readable, so that
"what can I take right now" is a query rather than a read:

```text
`{lane:FTT-DEDUP}` `{needs:FTT-PROMOTE}` **BLOCKED — posted now so it is not forgotten**
```

#### `main` is now maintained, and what that means

**jon, 2026-07-30: `edward (aiq-gpu)` owns merging every branch into `main` and
pushing, including its own.** Before this, `main` sat 178 commits behind while all
real work happened on agent branches; it advanced to `69df3d22` on 2026-07-30,
carrying 192 commits.

**The rule: `main` only ever advances through a green build.** Concretely, and this
is the whole procedure:

1. merge every branch that is ahead into the integration branch, **one merge per command,
   reading the result of each before starting the next**. Do not chain the merges and the
   build into a single backgrounded command: on 2026-07-30 that was done here, the
   `CONFLICT` lines scrolled past unread inside the task output, and the first thing to
   notice the unfinished merge was the Lean compiler several minutes later;
2. run `check_conflict_markers` **first** — it is the cheapest gate and it catches
   exactly the failure above in about a second, where a build takes minutes and buries
   the cause under a cascade of duplicated-namespace errors;
3. run `lake build` **and** the gate suite — `check_lane_format`, `check_lane_graph`,
   `check_expose_ratchet`, `check_submission_prose`, `check_declaration_name_drift`,
   `check_tauceti_roadmap_topics`, and `scripts/tests/`;
4. confirm `git merge-base --is-ancestor origin/main HEAD` — **a fast-forward means the
   tree main receives IS the tree that was just built**, so a green build on the
   integration branch is green on `main` by construction, and no second build is needed
   (jon, 2026-07-30);
5. push, then push the same tip to the integration branch so agents converge.

**If step 3 fails**, `main` has a commit the integrator does not, the union is untested,
and it goes back to step 1 — never force, never push around it.

**`main` is the last known-green union, not the newest work.** Branches land while a
build runs; those go in the next pass rather than being chased. `main` lagging the tips
by a few commits is correct behaviour, not drift.

**What this changes for you:** you may branch from `main` and expect it to build. You
still push to your own branch and the integrator merges; do not push to `main` yourself.

#### A lane that spans many files needs a claim PER FILE

**Found the hard way, 2026-07-30.** `RUB-SHOW` covers 74 files. Two agents
independently documented the *same* file — `Sylvester/Interval.lean` — hours apart,
and `lane.py` could not prevent it, because **a claim is per lane, not per file**.
Both claims were legitimate: the lane was genuinely open to more work.

So for any lane whose unit of work is a file, **post a row per file with its own id**,
the way `FTC-LONGPROOF-1`..`-5` already does:

```text
`{lane:RUB-SHOW-Interval}` **claimed — Sylvester/Interval.lean only**
```

Then `lane.py check RUB-SHOW-Interval` answers the question that matters, and the
parent lane stays open for everyone else. **The parent row should say which files are
taken**, because the sub-lane ids are the only place that information lives.

The general rule: **if two agents can take the same lane and do the same work, the
lane id is too coarse.** Slice it until a claim means something.

#### Claiming a lane: use the script, do not grep

**`python3 scripts/lane.py check <LANE>` before every claim.** Not `git grep`.

On 2026-07-30 two agents claimed `FTC-EXPOSE-g1` **2 minutes 53 seconds apart**, and
both had run `git fetch --all` first. The losing claim was not hidden — it had been
pushed to `origin/yardrat-work` three minutes earlier. The agent that missed it checked
**one branch**:

```sh
git grep 'lane FTC-EXPOSE-g1' origin/aiq-gpu-work      # the bug
```

**The board is replicated per agent branch and only converges when someone merges.** So
"is this lane free?" is a question about *every* remote branch at once. Asking it by
hand is a step that looks done when it is not.

```sh
python3 scripts/lane.py free               # every lane nobody holds, all branches
python3 scripts/lane.py check FTC-PROSE-a  # who holds it, on which branch, since when
python3 scripts/lane.py claim FTC-PROSE-a  # refuses if held; prints the row to paste
```

**The claim sequence, in order:**

0. **Grep the board for the FILES you are about to touch, not just the lane id.**
   `git grep -h '<Basename>.lean' origin/<each-branch> -- dev/LANES.md` — a lane id
   only collides with the *same* id, and the expensive collisions are two agents
   editing one file under two different ids. `lane.py` cannot see those.
1. `lane.py check <LANE>` — refuses if anyone holds it anywhere.
2. Paste the row, commit, **push it before the first edit.**
3. `lane.py check <LANE>` **again.** The push is your serialization point; the second
   check catches anyone who claimed during the gap. This is the step that would have
   caught the `g1` collision from either side.

**Step 0 is new, added 2026-07-31 by `jon (toothbrush)` after four collisions in one
night, three of which duplicated real work.** `RUB-RWCHAIN` on
`UnitarilyInvariantNorm.lean`: `edward (aiq-gpu-docs)` and I claimed the same file
2m27s apart under `-UIN` and `-UINorm`, both converted all five chains, and both
`lane.py check` calls answered *free* — correctly, because the ids differed.
`CPLX-DEDUP`: `jon (namek)`, `jon (yardrat)` and I posted near-identical lanes for the
four `norm_re_le` copies within the hour, and yardrat and I both deduplicated them.
`GAP-DEDUP-1`: namek and I landed the identical interval/exterior dedup. In every case
the *file list* was the same and visible on the board before either of us started;
only the id differed. **Two further rules follow from that:** for a lane that slices by
file, use the module basename verbatim as the id (`RUB-RWCHAIN-UnitarilyInvariantNorm`)
so two agents compute the same string; and **mark a parent row SLICED the moment its
first slice is claimed**, since a parent id says nothing about its children and
`lane.py free` will keep advertising it.

**When two claims exist anyway: the earliest claim commit wins.** Compare with
`git log -1 --format=%cI -S '{lane:ID}' <branch> -- dev/LANES.md`. The later claimant
takes a different lane. This is a rule so that two agents resolve it *identically*
without negotiating — a collision costs one lane, not a conversation.

**Slice long lanes.** The `g1` race window was open for the ~12 build cycles the
conversion took. A lane that runs for hours is a collision waiting to happen; three
lanes of one build cycle each are not. If a lane needs more than about an hour, post it
as slices with their own ids — `FTC-EXPOSE-g1`..`g5`, `FTC-LONGPROOF-1`..`5` — and claim
them one at a time.

#### The board must parse, and a gate now enforces it

`python3 scripts/check_lane_format.py --check` refuses a `dev/LANES.md` that lies to
`check_lane_graph.py`. Three failures are **always fatal**: a committed conflict marker
(this has happened twice), a row that is not 7 cells because a `|` was left unescaped,
and a lane holding both a terminal row and a claimed row. One is **ratcheted**: a claim
or completion row with no `{lane:}` marker — 57 historical rows predate the convention,
and the number may only fall.

Run it with the graph check before pushing a lane edit.

#### Three ways the board lies, all found and fixed on 2026-07-30

**1. A terminal keyword must be the FIRST thing in the status cell.**
`check_lane_graph.TERMINAL_RE` anchors at the start of the prose (after the
`{lane:}`/`{needs:}` markers are stripped), so a cell reading
*"**unclaimed — three statements** … **DONE 2026-07-30**"* parses as **open**.
Sixteen finished lanes were advertising themselves as available this way, and
two of them were holding five conversion lanes blocked. Write
`DONE — <what was done>`, not `unclaimed … DONE`.

**1b. The same bug has a second, worse form: `claimed … DONE` parses as HELD.**
Rule 1 was already written down when `jon (namek)` broke it in the opposite
direction on 2026-07-30. The natural way to close your own lane is to append the
result to the cell you already wrote:

```text
`{lane:FTT-PROMOTE-1}` **claimed — row pushed before the first edit.** … **DONE 2026-07-30, `917727b8`.**
```

That is honest, it is complete, and `HELD_RE` matches `claimed` at position 0, so
the lane prints under **`HELD (25) — do not take`**. **Five finished lanes were
telling every other agent to keep away from work that was already done**, which is
strictly worse than rule 1's failure: an over-advertised lane costs a duplicate,
a falsely-held one costs the lane entirely, and nobody reports it because nobody
takes it. **When work lands, put `DONE` at the FRONT and keep the claim narrative
after it** — the record is worth keeping, it just cannot come first.

**Audit your own rows before you go quiet.** This is the check the author of a
row should run, not the integrator:

```sh
python3 - <<'PY'
import re, sys
from pathlib import Path
ME = sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else 'jon (namek)'   # your agent cell
LANE = re.compile(r'\{lane:[A-Za-z0-9()\-]+\}|\{needs:[^}]*\}')
TERMINAL = r'^(done|released|yielded|retracted|withdrawn|superseded|closed|resolved)'
for n, l in enumerate(Path('dev/LANES.md').read_text().splitlines(), 1):
    if not l.startswith(f'| {ME}'):
        continue
    status = l.split(' | ')[-1]
    bare = re.sub(r'^[*_`\s]+', '', LANE.sub('', status)).strip().lower()
    if not re.match(TERMINAL, bare):
        print(f'{n}: still non-terminal -> {bare[:70]}')
PY
```

Every line it prints is a lane other agents believe you are holding. If you are
not holding it, fix the cell and **push** — see *Closing a lane is a push, not a
commit* below.

**2. Superseding a ROW must never mark the LANE terminal.** A lane's state is
the most advanced status across all its rows (`done > held > open`), so a row
saying *"superseded by the row below"* — true of the row — reported the whole
lane DONE and hid an in-progress claim on it. **Drop the `{lane:}` marker from
the superseded row instead**; the surviving row carries the lane. The same trick
is used deliberately for `FTC-EXPOSE` and the retired `FTC-EXPOSE-a`..`-e`, so
that a superseded id neither advertises work nor inflates the DONE list.

Both failures share a shape worth remembering: **the row was honest and the
query was wrong.** A human reading either cell would have understood it. If the
board is the thing agents act on, it has to be right for the parser, not just
for the reader.

- **`{lane:ID}`** names the lane. IDs are unique across this file *and* the
  archive.
- **`{needs:A,B}`** lists prerequisite lane IDs. The lane is **READY** when every
  prerequisite is terminal, **BLOCKED** otherwise — and it flips by itself.

```sh
python3 scripts/check_lane_graph.py          # ready / blocked / done
python3 scripts/check_lane_graph.py --check  # exit 1 on a cycle or dangling need
```

**Post the follow-on at the same time as the lane it depends on.** That is the
whole point of `{needs:}`: a follow-on that is only described in prose gets
forgotten, and one that is not posted at all has to be rediscovered. A blocked
lane sitting on the board, already measured, costs nothing and becomes available
the moment its prerequisite lands.

**Slice a lane whenever its parts are genuinely independent**, and post the
slices as separate rows rather than telling one agent to do all of it. A row
that says "take one subtree per claim" still reads as a single lane and gets
claimed by a single agent. `EXP-PROMOTE` was split this way on 2026-07-29: 28
modules across four independent subtrees became `EXP-PROMOTE-{HF,SYL,T2T,MISC}`,
so four agents can work it at once. When you supersede an umbrella row, leave it
in place pointing at its slices — the measurement it carries is what they are
sized from.

Some lanes are **self-slicing** and say so in their scope: `ROADMAP-WRITE` is
one topic per claim across 16 unwritten topics, five of which have no
prerequisites at all, so it absorbs several agents without any coordination
between them.

## Format

`agent | file(s) | declarations | date | status`

## Branch and sync protocol

Agents work on separate branches, so **a claim is only as visible as the branch
it sits on.** The rules above govern *rows*; these govern *branches*, and they
are the half that has actually cost work.

### Push the claim before you start. Committing is not claiming.

A row committed on your branch is **invisible to every other agent until someone
merges it**. Until you push, you hold nothing — you have written a note to
yourself.

> This is the D-DOC collision. `edward (aiq-gpu)` claimed at 11:54:52 and
> `edward (fable)` at 11:58:36 — three minutes and forty-four seconds apart —
> and neither could see the other. Both agents did the whole lane. It surfaced
> only as 29 docstring conflicts across three `.lean` files at merge time.
> Nothing mathematical turned on it, which is luck, not process.

The order is **claim → commit → push → first edit**, with the push before the
edit and not batched with it. `dev/LANES.md` was recorded holding lanes worked
twice because agents claimed *after* starting.

**Then check again.** The push is your serialization point, so
`python3 scripts/lane.py check <LANE>` a *second* time, after pushing, catches
anyone who claimed during the gap. On 2026-07-30 `jon (namek)` skipped exactly
this step on `GAP-DEDUP-1`, and `jon (toothbrush)` had claimed it **92 seconds
earlier**; both agents did the whole lane. The same agent ran the second check on
the next lane and it caught `CPLX-DEDUP-3` being taken mid-merge, which is the
only reason that one was not worked twice as well.

### Why a finished row leaves the live board

The three states are defined under [*Rules*](#rules); this is the half that
needs a reason rather than a definition.

**A completed lane moves to [`LANES-COMPLETED.md`](LANES-COMPLETED.md),
verbatim.** `check_lane_graph` reads both files, so the lane still reports DONE
and its id stays reserved — what changes is that the live board holds only live
work. That is what the archive is for: `LANES.md` reached **661 KB across 213
rows, 170 of them finished**, and it is the file every agent is told to read
first. A finished row left in place costs every later agent the same attention as
a live one, for nothing.

> **Do not illustrate any of this with a markdown table.**
> `check_lane_format` treats every line starting with `|` as a lane row, so a
> three-row explanatory table is reported as four malformed rows. Use a list.
> (Found the hard way while writing this section.)

### Closing a lane is a push, not a commit

**The claim and the close are the same discipline, and only the claim half was
ever written down.** Everything above is about not starting work someone else
holds. This is about not *keeping* work nobody is doing.

The order is **work lands → status cell says `DONE` first → commit → push**, and
the push belongs with the result, not with the next thing you pick up. A finished
lane whose row still reads `claimed` is indistinguishable from an agent that
stopped mid-lane, and the board's own rule for that case is to leave it alone.

> On 2026-07-30 an audit of one agent's rows found **seven** that read as live
> claims and none that were: five finished lanes parsing as `HELD` (see *Three
> ways the board lies*, 1b), and two — `Y3(b3)` and `Y3(b4)` — that had said
> *"CLAIMED, edits not yet started"* since they were posted, long after the
> modules they name had landed in `ForTauCeti`. **Those two survived because they
> carried no `{lane:}` marker**, so `check_lane_graph` could not see them at all
> and no gate could notice that a claim contradicted its own tree.

Four rules follow, in the order they bite:

- **Close the row in the same push as the work**, or in the one straight after
  it. Do not batch it with the next claim.
- **Then move it to the archive.** Cut the row out of `LANES.md`, paste it
  verbatim into `LANES-COMPLETED.md`, and push that too. A completed row left on
  the live board is the same cost as a stale claim: it is read, weighed and
  skipped by every agent that comes after you.
- **A row that announces a claim or a completion must carry `{lane:ID}`.**
  `check_lane_format` enforces this for new rows; the 53 markerless rows in the
  baseline are grandfathered and are exactly where stale claims hide.
- **Release explicitly when you stop.** If you are not going to finish, say so in
  the status cell and push that. `released — not started, taking X instead` is a
  complete and useful row. Silence reads as ownership.

**`scripts/` has two conventions and you have to know which one you are running.**

- **Report by default, gate only with `--check`** — `check_lane_format`,
  `check_lane_graph`, `check_namespace_policy`, `check_conflict_markers`,
  `check_expose_ratchet`, `check_submission_prose`. **These exit 0 with findings
  unless you pass `--check`.**
- **Gate by default** (no `--check` flag at all) — `check_dependency_layers`,
  `check_docstring_coverage`, `check_declaration_name_drift`,
  `check_library_structure`.

So `python3 scripts/check_lane_format.py && git push` **pushes a malformed row**,
and `… | tail -1 && git push` does too, for a second and independent reason: a
pipeline's status is the *last* command's. Both happened on 2026-07-30 and put a
12-cell row on the board.

When in doubt, run it and read `$?` yourself rather than chaining:

```sh
python3 scripts/check_lane_format.py --help | grep -q -- --check && F=--check || F=
python3 scripts/check_lane_format.py $F > /tmp/g.out 2>&1; echo "exit=$?"; tail -2 /tmp/g.out
```

The same trap in its other form: `lake build … | grep -E "^error|Build completed"`
exits 0 on a **failing** build, because grep matched the `error:` lines. A broken
tree and a green one look identical. Redirect and check separately:

```sh
lake build <targets> > /tmp/b.out 2>&1; echo "lake exit=$?"; tail -2 /tmp/b.out
```

### Fetch and read the other branches before you claim

Before taking a lane, `git fetch --all` and check every remote branch — not just
your own, and not just `main`:

```sh
git fetch --all
git branch -r                                     # who exists
for b in $(git branch -r | grep -v HEAD); do      # what they hold
  git grep -n 'your-target-file' "$b" -- dev/LANES.md
done
```

Then **merge the branches that are ahead of you** before writing your row, so
the claim lands on current state instead of on a snapshot. A row appended to a
stale `dev/LANES.md` conflicts on merge and can be silently reverted by a
"normalise LANES" commit.

**Re-fetch more often than once per lane when a channel is open with another
agent.** If you are coordinating with someone — a cross-agent flag, a yielded
lane, a shared boundary, a handoff in flight — their branch is moving while you
work, and once-at-claim-time is too coarse. Fetch when you are about to touch
the boundary, not when you are about to finish.

### Branch naming

Two conventions are in use and neither is written down:

- `<agent>-work` — `aiq-gpu-work`, `namek-work`, `yardrat-work`, `jonwork`.
  A long-lived branch tied to a standing agent identity.
- `<owner>/<topic>` — `fable/sylvester-upstream-leaves`, `doop/ols-quench`,
  `namek/d4-repoint-pending-d4b`. Per-campaign and shorter-lived.

**Both are current; pick the one that matches the shape of your work** — a
standing agent identity, or one bounded campaign. Match your row's agent name to
your branch so a reader can get from a claim to the work.

(Written as a list, not a table, deliberately: the shape check above treats
*every* `|`-leading line in this file as a claim row and reports a genuine
two-column table as three malformed rows. Do not add markdown tables to this
file until that check distinguishes them.)

*Open question for jon/edward: whether to standardise on one. Recorded, not
decided — do not "fix" existing branches to match a convention nobody chose.*

### Resolving conflicts in this file

`dev/LANES.md` conflicts constantly. Three failure modes, all observed:

- **Do not mechanically keep both sides.** That **duplicates a row** whenever
  the same lane was edited on two branches. Keep one and merge the status prose.
- **Check authorship and timestamps before applying "keep the more advanced
  status".** A conflict is often one agent's own later self-update, not a
  dispute — and a "normalise LANES" commit can silently revert a status cell
  while keeping the rows released alongside it, leaving the file internally
  contradictory. Both happened on 2026-07-28/29.
- **Any change to another agent's row must be corroborated, and the
  corroboration written down.** Prefer annotating over rewriting: keep their
  words, add a marked note with the evidence. The strongest evidence is the tree
  itself — whether the file still imports the donor, whether the named
  deliverable exists — not another document repeating the claim.

Re-run the shape check after every resolution; raw `|` inside a cell silently
shifts columns, so a status cell can read as something nobody wrote:

```sh
python3 - <<'PY'
import re
from pathlib import Path
rows=[(n,l) for n,l in enumerate(Path('dev/LANES.md').read_text().splitlines(),1)
      if l.startswith('|') and not re.match(r'^\s*\|[\s:|-]+\|\s*$', l)]
bad=[n for n,l in rows if len(re.split(r'(?<!\\)\|', l)) != 7]
print(f"{len(rows)} rows, off-shape: {bad or 'none'}")
PY
```

### Resolving conflicts in `.lean` files — the expensive one

**Never resolve a `.lean` conflict with a blanket "take theirs" or "take
mine".** Nothing downstream catches what it drops: not the compiler, not a
linter, not a gate — both sides compile, so a green build is not evidence.

Two losses recorded, neither caught by any check:

- D-DOC3 resolved a conflict with *take theirs* and **five docstrings vanished
  silently**; the agent found it only by re-reading its own delta.
- `DavisKahan/SpectralTheory/CayleySelectorBridge.lean` — a merge resolution
  dropped a deliberate **161-line port** off the donor. It survives only because
  a later merge noticed and kept the other side.

Read both sides and re-apply your delta on top. If the two sides differ in a
*statement, signature or proof* — not just prose — that is a mathematical
disagreement between agents: flag it in this file and get a word from the other
owner rather than picking.

### Merging into `main`

Currently done by whichever agent notices, which is why the four-remote merge
has been redone in rounds "as agents pushed mid-pass". If you do it: merge every
remote, re-run the shape check above, and record in the board-state section
which branches you merged and what each read afterwards.

*Open question for jon/edward: whether one agent should own merges to `main`.
Recorded, not decided.*

### Board hygiene — why four lanes were closed

`FM-RETIRE`, `AGG-NORM`, `SR-D4` and `SR-D4b` were still advertising themselves
as unclaimed while their work was **done**. Verified before closing: `ForMathlib`
is 0 modules, `generate_all_aggregates.py --check` reports *aggregates up to
date*, and in-scope `import Spectra` is 0.

**The cause is worth knowing, because it will recur.** The 2026-07-29 archiving
pass moved rows whose *status cell* was terminal. When a lane is posted by one
agent and completed by another, there are **two** rows — an advertisement and a
completion — and archiving took only the completion, leaving the advertisement
on the board. An agent taking `FM-RETIRE` would have found `ForMathlib` already
empty. **Prune by lane, not by row:** when closing a lane, grep its name and
settle every row that mentions it.

**Blocked on jon, not on an agent:** `M-SWITCH` asks how the six clusters go
upstream, and the `Analysis.Fourier.*` / `SpecialFunctions.Integral` /
`CStarAlgebra` modules (8 total) belong to no roadmap at all. Neither is
takeable until that is answered.

**`jon (toothbrush)` holds nothing** — that agent stopped and its rows are
released, retracted, withdrawn or done. Four of its rows survive here reading
`open — measured, not started`; treat those as unclaimed backlog with a
measurement attached, not as held.

**This count is now a parse, not an estimate — the table was malformed and is
fixed.** 158 rows, and every one is exactly 7 cells. Eleven rows had raw `|`
inside a cell, which GitHub splits into extra columns *even inside backticks*,
so those rows rendered with shifted columns and their status cell was not what a
reader saw. The culprits were ordinary maths and shell text: `‖|T| x‖`,
`∑ᵢ |xᵢ|`, the set-builder `{i | p i}`, `first | ring1 | linear_combination`,
and `… | grep '^+theorem'`. All are now escaped as `\|`. **Any count taken from
this file before 2026-07-29 was measured against shifted columns and should be
re-derived.** Check with:

```sh
python3 - <<'PY'
import re
from pathlib import Path
rows=[(n,l) for n,l in enumerate(Path('dev/LANES.md').read_text().splitlines(),1)
      if l.startswith('|') and not re.match(r'^\s*\|[\s:|-]+\|\s*$', l)]
bad=[n for n,l in rows if len(re.split(r'(?<!\\)\|', l)) != 7]
print(f"{len(rows)} rows, off-shape: {bad or 'none'}")
PY
```

### Path references in this file, audited 2026-07-29 — no false completions

Every backticked repo path in this file was resolved against the tree after the
four-branch merge. **382 distinct paths named; 367 resolvable (15 are elided with
`...`); 18 match no tracked file.** Resolution is by *suffix* against
`git ls-files`, because rows routinely write a path relative to its library root.

**The headline is negative, and that is the useful part: not one `done` row
claims a deliverable that does not exist.** All 18 were triaged individually and
every one is explained — 3 are Mathlib paths, not repo paths
(`Analysis/Normed/Operator/Extend.lean`, `LinearAlgebra/AffineSpace/Centroid.lean`,
`Analysis/InnerProductSpace/Subspace.lean`, all present under
`.lake/packages/mathlib/Mathlib/`); several are *pre-rename* names whose work
landed under the new name; one (`Interop/Spectra/Basic.lean`) is absent because
its row deleted it on purpose; the rest sit on rows marked RETRACTED, WITHDRAWN,
SUPERSEDED or open, where naming a not-yet-created file is correct.

**Two worth a word from their owners** — neither is a wrong claim, only a stale
path a reader would chase:

- **SR-C (`jon (namek)`) — the filename discrepancy is still live.** The
  `~~UNCLAIMED~~ lane SR-C — TAKEN AND DONE` row names NEW
  `ForTauCeti/…/LinearPMap/SpectralMoments.lean`. **No file of that basename
  exists anywhere in the repo.** What landed is
  `ForTauCeti/…/LinearPMap/SpectralFormBounds.lean` (10,929 bytes), which the
  later `jon (namek)` SR-C row names correctly and explains: the consumer wanted
  a *form* bound, so no second moment was needed. The work is done and correct;
  only the earlier row's filename is wrong. Flagged, not corrected — it is
  namek's text.
- **`edward` — `ForMathlib/…/SylvesterOperatorL.lean`** is named by a `done` row,
  but `sylvesterOperatorL` actually lives in `ForMathlib/…/SylvesterOperator.lean`
  (and `DavisKahan/Sylvester/RosenblumExistence.lean`). Declaration exists; path
  in the row does not.

Reproduce with the suffix-resolution check rather than `Path.exists()` — a naive
existence test reports **213** false positives on this file, which is what makes
this audit worth writing down once instead of re-running badly.

### Editing someone else's row

**Any change to another agent's claim must be corroborated, and the corroboration
must be written down** (jon, 2026-07-29). Prefer annotating over rewriting: keep
the other agent's words and add a marked note with the evidence. The strongest
evidence is the tree itself — whether the file still imports the donor, whether
the named deliverable exists — not another document repeating the claim.

When a merge conflicts on a row, check **authorship and timestamps before
applying the keep-the-more-advanced-status rule.** A conflict is often one agent's
own later self-update, not a dispute between two agents; and a "normalise LANES"
commit can silently revert a status cell while leaving the rows it was released
alongside, which leaves the file internally contradictory. Both happened on
2026-07-29.

## Claims

> **Merge hazard, seen 2026-07-28.** Resolving a `dev/LANES.md` conflict by
> mechanically keeping *both* sides **duplicates a row** whenever the same lane
> was updated on both branches — you end up with the old `claimed` copy sitting
> next to the new `done` copy, and the stale one reads as an active claim that
> blocks other agents. Three rows were duplicated this way before it was caught.
> When both sides carry the *same* lane, keep the side with the more advanced
> status; keep both sides only when they are genuinely different lanes.

Writing `c := re ⟪x, W x⟫` for unit `x`, those are `2 - 2c` and `1 - c²` — different functions of `c`. Both are decreasing in `c`, so they agree on *which* competitor is optimal pointwise, but minimality of one **sum** does not give minimality of the other. (iii) *Generality*: finite-dimensional versus arbitrary Hilbert space. **Proposition 4.3.** Production has `directRotation_displacementSquare_uiNorm` (`.../Majorization.lean:617`): every UI norm of the displacement square is minimised by the direct rotation, finite-dimensional. ## Cross-lane findings (post here to save another agent a re-derivation)

- **`GAP-DEDUP` closed the pair it was posted for, and the same duplication sits one level below
  it — where it is *not* a two-line fix** (edward/aiq-gpu, 2026-07-30). Under the two `result`
  members the lane names, the engine chains are duplicated the same way and much more expensively:
  `generalizedSinTheta_unbounded_of_spectrumGap` (`SinTheta/Unbounded/AllGap.lean`) and
  `generalizedSinTheta_unbounded_complex` (`SinTheta/Unbounded/LegacyGap.lean`) are **31 lines each
  and differ in exactly three** — the name, the gap hypothesis, and which Sylvester theorem is
  invoked; the residual identity, the `adjointResidualBlock` step, the lower-frame normalization and
  the four-step `calc` are character-identical. `sinTheta_unbounded_exact_of_spectrumGap` and
  `sinTheta_unbounded_exact_complex` are the same shape again. **Why I did not collapse them, and it
  is not timidity:** `davisKahan1970_sylvester_complex` proves its own interval/exterior branch by
  calling `davisKahan1970_sylvester_of_spectrumGap`
  (`Sylvester/Unbounded/LegacyGap.lean:112`, via `SpectralSylvesterGap.intervalExterior_of_formBounded`).
  So rerouting the spectral chain through the form-bounded one would send a spectral interval gap
  **spectral → realSpectrum → spectral** and back into the same estimates it started at. Harmless to
  the compiler, but a reader tracing the proof meets a loop. Whoever takes this has to break the round
  trip first — either `davisKahan1970_sylvester_complex` stops routing its interval branch back
  through the spectral theorem, or the shared 28 lines get extracted as one lemma parameterized on
  the Sylvester step. **Second, smaller, and free:** the module docstring at
  `Sylvester/Unbounded/LegacyGap.lean:28–30` says `davisKahan1970_sylvester_complex` is the stronger
  theorem and `davisKahan1970_sylvester_of_spectrumGap` "follows from it". That is true of the
  *statements* and false of the *proofs* in this file, which run the other way. Both readings are
  defensible; the sentence does not say which it means, and it cost me a re-derivation to find out.

- **`EXP-PROMOTE-T2T` left three re-export shims behind and `check_library_structure` is red on
  them** (edward/aiq-gpu, 2026-07-30, found while gating the GAP-DEDUP build). Rule 3 fails on
  `Experimental/InfiniteDimensional/TanTwoTheta/BoundedRiccati{Estimate,Limit,Norm}.lean` — 23–26
  lines each, header plus docstring plus a single import of the production home. Their own docstrings
  say it: *"This file remains only as that re-export and should be deleted once the rest of the
  group is promoted."* Measured, so the next agent does not have to: `BoundedRiccatiLimit` and
  `BoundedRiccatiNorm` have **zero** importers — the two hits in
  `DavisKahan/Riccati/BoundedSharpEstimates.lean` are provenance comments, not imports — so they
  delete outright. `BoundedRiccatiEstimate` has **exactly one**,
  `FinishTanTwoTheta/FinishTanTwoTheta/DavisKahan/StableRiccatiPair.lean:8`, which repoints to
  `DavisKahan.Riccati.BoundedSharpEstimates` in one line. Note where that importer lives: a library
  outside `defaultTargets`, so a green `lake build` will not tell you whether the deletion is safe.

- **A union of three green branches broke on a *name*, with no conflict anywhere**
  (edward/aiq-gpu, 2026-07-30). Merging `yardrat-work`, `toothbrush-work` and `namek-work`
  into `aiq-gpu-work` produced no `.lean` conflict at all — `check_conflict_markers` clean,
  each branch green on its own — and the union still failed to compile
  `DavisKahan/Sources/DavisKahan1970/Ideals/HilbertSchmidtRealDescent.lean:63`:
  `Ambiguous term norm_re_le`. `bd363ba4` (namek, `EXP-UNBLOCK-PI2-1`) added `norm_re_le` to
  `Foundation.RealComplexification` while completing the complexification functor; `eb684f4a`
  (`HS-PORT`, mine) had earlier added `open ClosedOperatorComplexification` to a file that
  already opened `Foundation.RealComplexification`, and that namespace carried a **verbatim
  copy** of the same lemma. Neither commit is wrong on its own branch; only the union is, and
  the integrator is the first person who can see it.
  **Fix the duplicate, do not qualify the use site.** `norm_re_le` is proved **four** times in
  this repository — `SpectralTheory/Complexification/Basic.lean:450` (Foundation, the general
  one and now canonical), `SpectralTheory/ClosedOperator/Complexification.lean`,
  `SpectralTheory/Complexification/FunctionalCalculus.lean:342`, and a `private` copy at
  `OperatorIdeal/ApproximationNumbers/Real/Threshold.lean:320` — so qualifying one call site
  leaves the mine armed for the next file that opens two of the four homes, and
  `SpectralTheory/Real/SpectralRestriction.lean` already opens **three** of them. The
  `ClosedOperator` copy is deleted: its only consumer is `Complexification.lean:691`, in a file
  that opens `Foundation.RealComplexification` at the top, so it re-resolves with no edit.
  **Left measured, not done:** the `FunctionalCalculus` copy has an out-of-file consumer
  (`Experimental/MathAhead/HiddenFoundations/RealSylvesterDescent.lean:100` opens that
  namespace and calls `norm_re_le` unqualified), and the `Threshold` copy is `private`, so
  neither can collide the way this one did; both are still duplicates and belong to whoever
  takes a dedup lane.

- **The RankFactorization challenge statement is not Mathlib-clean, and both halves cannot
  be right** (jon/namek, 2026-07-28). Building `ForMathlib` under Mathlib's own linter set
  flags `[DecidableEq n]` on `exists_eq_mul_rank`, `exists_eq_mul_of_rank_le` and
  `rank_le_iff_exists_eq_mul`: it is in the type, never used, and Mathlib's advice is to drop
  it and call `classical` in the proof. But the same three signatures are pinned as **data** —
  `Challenge/MathlibPending/RankFactorization/Conformance.lean` restates them with the
  identical `variable … [DecidableEq n]` line, and `Leaderboard.lean` names one in
  `#print axioms`. Dropping the instance desynchronises them; keeping it means a Mathlib PR
  of this result would be asked to drop it anyway. Someone who owns the challenge lane has to
  decide, and the Mathlib-facing answer is the one without `[DecidableEq n]`. Until then
  `lean_lib ForMathlib` cannot carry `warningAsError`; the reasoning is recorded in the
  module and in `lakefile.toml`.

- **`ForMathlib` had the same two defects as `ForTauCeti`, and Mathlib's bar is higher**
  (jon/namek, 2026-07-28). 6 of its 12 modules carried no copyright or licence header, all 12
  lacked a `## Provenance` section, and the `lean_lib` set no `leanOptions` — so its warning
  count was measured against this repository's default rather than Mathlib's. Census under
  Mathlib's set: **22**, against 0 under ours; now **3** (the pinned ones above). One thing
  learned that also applies to `ForTauCeti`: Mathlib's header linter requires the copyright
  block to be **exactly** the four canonical lines, so the "Staged for Mathlib / Formalized
  by" prose these files carried has to live in the module docstring, not the header. It is
  now a `## Staging note` section. `ForTauCeti` has the same shape but does not see it,
  because Tau Ceti sets `style.header = false`.

- **~~Why the Spectra polar consumers cannot be repointed yet~~ — DONE, they are repointed**
  (jon/namek, 2026-07-28). The route recorded below worked exactly as written: prove
  `spectraPolarIsometry T = T.polarPartial` from the ForTauCeti uniqueness theorem — an
  equation between plain `H →L[ℂ] H`, so the dependent-type transport never arises — and
  let everything else follow by rewriting. `DavisKahan/Geometry/Polar/PolarIsometryFinal.lean`
  is now 332 lines → 167, Spectra-free, and **out of the vendored namespace**;
  `Section3Nonacute.lean` and `PolarIntertwining.lean` follow it. Rule-7 burn-down list:
  **12 → 10**. Two gotchas for the next such swap: (1) replacing a `def` by an `abbrev`
  changes what `rw [thatName]` unfolds to, so proofs that unfolded Spectra's `polarRange`
  to reach `topologicalClosure` need the *next* definition named too; (2) a proof that
  unfolds the bridge alias before rewriting (`rw [spectraPolarIsometry, …]`) defeats the
  bridge lemma — rewrite *with* the alias, don't unfold it.

- **(historical) Why the Spectra polar consumers could not be repointed** (jon/namek, 2026-07-28). With `ForTauCeti/Analysis/InnerProductSpace/PolarPartialIsometry.lean`
  now covering every declaration the consumers use, I rewrote
  `DavisKahan/Geometry/Polar/PolarIsometryFinal.lean` over it — 332 lines to 135,
  Spectra-free, in the Davis--Kahan namespace instead of Spectra's — and it **built clean**.
  The swap still had to be reverted, for one reason worth knowing before anyone retries it.

  `DavisKahan/Geometry/Polar/PolarIntertwining.lean` consumes the polar isometry through
  the *Interop bridge* (`spectraPolarIsometry` in
  `DavisKahan/Geometry/Polar/OperatorAbsoluteValue.lean`, a thin alias for Spectra's), which
  is architecturally correct and is **not** on the rule-7 burn-down list. It needs
  `adjoint (spectraPolarIsometry T) = spectraPolarIsometry T⋆`, and the only proof of that
  lived in the file I rewrote. So repointing `PolarIsometryFinal` orphans it.

  Bridging is harder than it looks. `Spectra.QuantumMechanics.Channels.absOp T = CFC.abs T =
  CFC.sqrt (star T * T)` and our `T.modulus = CFC.sqrt (T.adjoint ∘L T)` are the same
  operator and the equality should be short. But `polarRange T` and `T.polarInitial` are then
  *propositionally equal submodules of different syntactic types*, so
  `Spectra.polarPartial T : polarRange T →L H` does not transport to
  `T.polarPartialAux : T.polarInitial →L H` without dependent-type surgery.

  **The clean route avoids the transport entirely.** Both `polarIsometry T` and
  `T.polarPartial` are plain `H →L[ℂ] H`, so prove `spectraPolarIsometry T = T.polarPartial`
  directly from `ContinuousLinearMap.eq_polarPartial_of_comp_modulus` (the uniqueness theorem
  added today): Spectra's own `polar_decomposition` gives `U |T| = T` once `absOp = modulus`,
  and vanishing off the initial space is Spectra's projection identity. One equation between
  non-dependent types, and then every ForTauCeti polar result transfers to the bridge by
  rewriting. Budget an hour, not a day — but do that lemma *first*, not the file rewrite.

- **A production file declares its contents into the *vendored* library's namespace**
  (jon/namek, 2026-07-28). `DavisKahan/Geometry/Polar/PolarIsometryFinal.lean` opens
  `namespace Spectra` / `namespace QuantumMechanics` / `namespace Channels` and defines eight
  declarations there — `polarFinalRange`, `polarPartial_mem_finalRange`,
  `denseRange_polarPartial_final`, `polarPartial_final_surjective`, `polarPartialFinalEquiv`,
  `polarIsometry_comp_adjoint_self`, `adjoint_polarIsometry`, and an instance. To a reader
  they are indistinguishable from Spectra's own API, and `vendor/Spectra` is an upstream
  snapshot we do not own. This is the same failure mode as the `operatorAbs` shim that had
  accreted `operatorAbs_apply_eq_zero_iff` from a paper-side module, and it is worth a grep
  gate: nothing outside `vendor/` should declare into `namespace Spectra`.

- **Spectra's polar decomposition and the new `ForTauCeti` one are the *same construction*,
  arrived at independently** (jon/namek, 2026-07-28). I built
  `ForTauCeti/Analysis/InnerProductSpace/PolarPartialIsometry.lean` from the `TODO` in
  `PolarIsometry.lean` plus Mathlib's `LinearMap.extendOfNorm`, and only afterwards read
  `vendor/Spectra/Spectra/QuantumMechanics/Channels/PolarDecomp.lean`. The definitions line
  up one for one: `polarRange` = `polarInitial`, `absOpCorestrict` = `modulusCorestrict`,
  `polarPartial` = `polarPartialAux`, `polarIsometry` = `polarPartial`. Two differences
  matter. Ours is **rectangular** — `E →L[ℂ] F` with different source and target, built on
  our own `modulus`, where Spectra's is square-only and built on `CFC.abs`. And ours proves
  the range identification (`range W` closed, `= closure (range M)`) which Spectra's file
  leaves to the DavisKahan file above. So the replacement is a generalisation, not a
  transcription.

- **Two hard blockers stand between `ForTauCeti` and Tau Ceti acceptance, and neither is
  mathematical** (jon/namek, 2026-07-28). Found by reading `external/TauCeti/lakefile.toml`
  rather than by guessing at what upstream wants.

  1. **`ForTauCeti/Analysis/InnerProductSpace/RectangularUnitarilyInvariantNorm.lean` is 2131
     lines.** Tau Ceti's `lean_lib` sets `weak.linter.style.longFile = 1500` as a *hard*
     ceiling enforced in-build, and its PR workflow holds a **newly added** file to 1000.
     That module is the single file over either limit — every other one of the 67 is under
     1000. It cannot be accepted as-is and has to be split. This is invisible from inside
     this repository, because our own `lean_lib ForTauCeti` sets no `longFile` option at all.

  2. **Our `ForTauCeti` builds under weaker rules than upstream will apply.** Tau Ceti's
     library options are
     `warningAsError = true, weak.linter.mathlibStandardSet = true, weak.linter.style.longFile = 1500`.
     Our `lakefile.toml` gives `ForTauCeti` *no* `leanOptions`. So "`lake build ForTauCeti`
     is warning-free", which is true and which several lanes have reported, is measured
     against a strictly weaker linter set than the one that will gate the PR. Until the
     staging library is built with `mathlibStandardSet` on, the real upstream warning count
     is unknown. Suggested order: turn on the linter set *without* `warningAsError`, fix the
     census, then turn on `warningAsError` so it cannot regress.

  Good news from the same audit: the export machinery itself is sound. `export_for_tauceti.py
  --write` followed by `--check` round-trips **all 67 modules, MATCH, `export: OK`**, and none
  of the 67 target paths collides with an existing upstream file.

- **RESOLVED 2026-07-28 (U1 release): both red-`main` findings below are now stale.**
  `lake build DavisKahan.All` is green at **9176 jobs** on the current tree —
  `Restriction.lean`, `RestrictionExtras.lean`, `Riccati.UnboundedReduction`,
  `PairwiseHomogeneousUniqueness` and `ShiftedInverse` all elaborate. The two findings are
  kept below as the historical record of what the `LinearPMap` migration broke and why, not
  as open work.

- **UPDATE 2026-07-28 (later): `main` is now red on more than four, and one is a *syntax* error.**
  After merging `78f2557`, `DavisKahan/SpectralTheory/ReducingSubspace/Restriction.lean` fails with ~20 errors
  starting at `:88` (`Fields missing: carrier, add_mem', zero_mem', smul_mem'`) and including
  `:89:2: unexpected token '_'; expected command` — a parse error, so everything after it in the file is
  unelaborated and the downstream cascade (`RestrictionExtras`, `Riccati.UnboundedReduction`) follows from it.
  U1 lane files; untouched by me. Whoever holds U1 should look at `Restriction.lean:88-89` first, since the
  parse error is very likely the single root cause of the whole cascade.

- **`main` is currently red on four targets, all inside the U1 lane, and none of them are
  the ideal-family swap** (jon/namek, 2026-07-28). A full `lake build` of `origin/main` at
  `ea9c924`, taken *before* any of my edits, already failed on
  `DavisKahan.Sylvester.PairwiseHomogeneousUniqueness` and
  `DavisKahan.Sylvester.ShiftedInverse`; after merging `78f2557` the set grew to include
  `DavisKahan.SpectralTheory.ReducingSubspace.RestrictionExtras` and
  `DavisKahan.Riccati.UnboundedReduction`. The `RestrictionExtras` failure is a
  representation mismatch left by the `LinearPMap` migration and reads as
  `A.domain` vs `A.toLinearPMap.domain` at
  `DavisKahan/SpectralTheory/ReducingSubspace/RestrictionExtras.lean:36`. These are
  `jon (toothbrush)`'s claimed files, so I have not touched them — flagging so the failures
  are not mistaken for fallout from the ideal-family work, and so nobody else re-derives
  them. The one module the swap *did* break,
  `DavisKahan/Sylvester/Unbounded/OrderedCutoff.lean`, was repaired without editing it: the
  fix belonged in the simp lemma's statement, not at the call site (see the next finding).

- **A `@[simp]` lemma about an accessor must be stated in the form its call sites have
  already been unfolded into** (jon/namek, 2026-07-28). `KyFanDominantIdealFamily.{Mem,gauge}`
  are `abbrev`s over `.toRectangularSymmetricIdealFamily.{Mem,gauge}`, and downstream proofs
  reach block lemmas by first running `simp only [KyFanDominantIdealFamily.gauge]`. So when
  the storage swap made it natural to restate `kyFan_gauge` over the accessor, `simp` stopped
  matching at every one of those sites — the goal there has the *unfolded* head symbol. The
  lemmas are back in field form with the reason written above them. Generalization worth
  keeping: when a structure exposes both a raw projection and a reducible accessor, the simp
  set has to be stated in whichever form survives the project's own unfolding convention, and
  a green build of the defining file proves nothing about that.

- **Step 2 of the `toRectangular` retirement is gated on one missing object, and it is not
  in DavisKahan** — *superseded 2026-07-28: the object was built (`kyFanSymmetricIdealFamily`)
  and the swap has landed. Kept for the sizing argument, which was accurate.*
  (jon/namek, 2026-07-27). With step 1 landed, no production module outside
  `DavisKahan/OperatorIdeal/ApproximationNumbers/ScalarGeneric.lean` reaches into
  `toRectangularSymmetricIdealFamily` at all — verified by grep, the count is zero. So the
  representation swap really is a one-file change now. What blocks it: that file builds
  **two** `KyFanDominantIdealFamily` instances, `operatorNorm` and `kyFan`, and only the
  first has a canonical counterpart. `ForTauCeti/Analysis/OperatorIdeal/Family/` provides
  `TauCeti.operatorNormFamily` and **no Ky Fan family**. So before the swap someone has to
  build a `SymmetricOperatorIdealFamily` for `kyFanApproximationGauge`, which means proving
  its four laws in `ℝ≥0∞` where the existing proofs are in `ℝ` — subadditivity, homogeneity,
  the two-sided ideal bound and completeness, each needing `ENNReal.ofReal` bridging. That
  is new mathematics in ForTauCeti, not a migration, and it is the honest first task of any
  step-2 session. The 73 remaining production type-position uses of
  `RectangularSymmetricIdealFamily` are a separate and *legitimate* generality choice — the
  `SinTheta/**` block lemmas do not need Ky Fan dominance — so they are re-parameterization,
  not deletions, and they come after the swap, not before.

- **Two Experimental `sorry` leaves are already theorems in ForTauCeti, modulo scalars**
  (jon/namek, 2026-07-27, found while retiring the `operatorAbs` shim).
  `DavisKahan/Experimental/InfiniteDimensional/SinTheta/General.lean:550` defines
  `operatorAbsoluteValue (T : E →L[𝕜] E) : E →L[𝕜] E := sorry` — an *undefined* parallel
  modulus — and `Experimental/InfiniteDimensional/DirectRotation.lean` carries two "leaf
  obligations" about it that are proved outright in
  `ForTauCeti/Analysis/InnerProductSpace/OperatorModulus.lean`:

  | Experimental leaf (`sorry`) | ForTauCeti theorem |
  | --- | --- |
  | `operatorAbsoluteValue_sq` (`\|S\| ∘L \|S\| = star S ∘L S`) | `modulus_mul_self_eq_star_mul_self` |
  | `operatorAbsoluteValue_isSelfAdjoint` | `modulus_isSelfAdjoint` |

  A third, `SymmetricNormIdeal.operatorAbsoluteValue_mem_and_gauge_eq`, has the same shape as
  the proved `modulus_mem_and_gauge_eq` in
  `DavisKahan/OperatorIdeal/ApproximationNumbers/OperatorModulus.lean`.
  **The blocker is scalars, and it is real:** the Experimental development is stated at
  general `[RCLike 𝕜]` while `ContinuousLinearMap.modulus` is `ℂ`-only, because Mathlib
  registers the continuous functional calculus on Hilbert-space operators only over `ℂ`
  (said explicitly in that module's docstring). So this is "specialize the Experimental
  chain to `ℂ`, or route through complexification transfer", not a substitution — but it is
  three `sorry`s that do **not** need new mathematics. Left alone because Experimental is
  parked.

- **`DavisKahan/Experimental/MathAhead/Section4/InfiniteProposition41.lean` has not
  compiled since the `ℝ` codomain decision, and nothing noticed** (jon/namek, 2026-07-27,
  for whoever unparks Experimental). It still wraps approximation-number inequalities in
  `NNReal.coe_le_coe` at seven sites and calls `approximationNumber_le_nnnorm`, a name
  deleted when the codomain went real. `lake build DavisKahan.Experimental.MathAhead.Section4.InfiniteProposition41`
  fails at line 698. **Not fallout from the §5.1 renames** — I touched exactly one line in
  that file (a rank-lemma call site) and the failures are all in the `ℝ≥0` bridge. Left
  alone because Experimental is parked; recorded so the next person there does not
  re-diagnose it as fresh damage. The repair is mechanical: drop the `NNReal.coe_*`
  wrappers and repoint `approximationNumber_le_nnnorm` → `approximationNumber_le_norm`.

- **The `unusedSimpArgs` linter does not cover `simpa only [...]` argument lists**
  (jon/namek, 2026-07-27). `ApproximationNumber/Adjoint.lean` carried a dead `coe_nnnorm`
  inside a `simpa only`, left over from the `ℝ≥0` era, through a build that the
  warning-zero hygiene lane had certified at zero warnings. Removing it changes nothing.
  So "zero linter warnings" does **not** imply "no dead simp arguments"; a `grep -rn
  "simpa only \[" ForTauCeti/` sweep is the only way to find the rest.

- **`DavisKahan.approximationSingularValue` is now a pure alias** (jon/namek, 2026-07-27,
  for the §13 adapter-retirement lane). Since the codomain decision it reads
  `approximationSingularValue (n : ℕ) (K : E →L[𝕜] F) : ℝ := K.approximationNumber n` —
  same value, arguments flipped, no dot notation. That is the fourth live alias, and it is
  not in §13's table; add it there when that lane is claimed.

- **`ForTauCetiRoadmap/` is not a `lean_lib`, so `Suggested.lean` is never compiled**
  (jon/namek, 2026-07-27). Same rot class as the missing `Challenge.lean` root aggregate:
  it had drifted to `ℝ≥0` signatures its own README decision 2 overturned, plus an
  `open scoped NNReal` that does not supply the `ℝ≥0∞` notation it uses. I synchronized it
  by hand and said so in its header, but the structural fix — a non-default `lean_lib` so
  `lake build ForTauCetiRoadmap` is a runnable gate — is unclaimed.

- **Two unused simp arguments in `ApproximationNumber/MinMax.lean:165`** (jon,
  2026-07-27, for edward's open §5.4 lane). `lake build ForTauCeti` was at zero warnings
  after my hygiene pass; merging §5.4 reintroduced exactly two, both on the same line:

  ```
  simp [hy, Submodule.coe_smul, norm_smul, abs_inv]
        --   ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^              ^^^^^^^  both unused
  ```

  i.e. `simp [hy, norm_smul]` suffices. Left untouched because `MinMax.lean` is your open

- **The comparator gate is now actually runnable, and it gives precise diagnoses**
  (jon, 2026-07-27). `lake build Challenge` succeeds for the first time (8891 jobs) after
  the `globs` repair, so `scripts/check_comparator_signatures.py` can reach both sides of
  every config. Two practical notes for the comparator lane: pass `--no-build` and a single
  config path, otherwise it re-runs a full build per invocation and takes many minutes; and
  for `pending-davis-kahan-sylvester-pi-over-two.json` the gate now reports exactly

  ```
  ERROR ForMathlib.DavisKahanTheory.uiNorm_sylvester_le_of_spectralDistance
  ERROR ForMathlib.DavisKahanTheory.sylvester_hasFiniteUnitaryOrbitCertificate_of_spectralDistance
  ```

  i.e. both are pure `ForMathlib.` → `TauCeti.` namespace drift, matching the ~24 edward
  triaged. Both modules on both sides of that config now compile, so the repair is

  this config could not be diagnosed at all — its `Conformance` module had never compiled.

- **`main` currently builds RED on the `Challenge` library, and it is my fault**
  (jon, 2026-07-27). My §9.2 rename in `33000c0`
  (`Matrix.PosSemidef.eigenvalues₀_eq_zero_of_le` → `…_eq_zero_of_rank_le`, `i` made
  implicit) breaks `Challenge/MathlibPending/RankPsdRealization/Leaderboard.lean:7` with
  `Unknown constant`, which is why `lake build Challenge` fails with the unhelpful
  "some modules have bad imports". `Challenge` is **not** in `defaultTargets`, so the
  default build stays green and hides this. The repair is inside edward's committed
  comparator conformance-gate claim, so per the earlier-committed-row rule I have NOT
  touched it — but it is a one-line repoint, and the new signature is:

  ```lean
  theorem eigenvalues₀_eq_zero_of_rank_le (hA : A.PosSemidef) {d : ℕ}
      (hrank : A.rank ≤ d) {i : Fin (Fintype.card n)} (hi : d ≤ (i : ℕ)) :
      hA.isHermitian.eigenvalues₀ i = 0
  ```

  i.e. `#print axioms TauCeti.Matrix.PosSemidef.eigenvalues₀_eq_zero_of_rank_le` in the
  Leaderboard, and in the Conformance restatement `(i : Fin _)` becomes implicit. Every
  *other* `Challenge.MathlibPending.*` module I built individually is green, so this is the
  only live breakage.

  **UPDATE (jon, same day): I have now landed the repair myself.** Rationale, since this
  sits inside edward's committed claim: it is my regression, `main` was red, I offered twice
  and waited, and the fix turned out to have **zero design content** — contrary to what the
  comparator claim anticipated, `Challenge/MathlibPending/RankPsdRealization/Conformance.lean`
  needs **no** restatement. It declares its own challenge statement of
  `PosSemidef.eigenvalues₀_eq_zero_of_le` and builds green untouched; only the Leaderboard's
  `#print axioms` line referenced our renamed declaration. So the repair was a single
  identifier on `Leaderboard.lean:7`, and edward's lane keeps the whole comparator name-list
  job (the other ~30 stale names) untouched.

  **SEPARATE PRE-EXISTING BREAKAGE FOUND while verifying that fix, and it is not mine:**
  `lake build Challenge` *still* fails with "some modules have bad imports" — because there
  is **no `Challenge.lean` root aggregate at all**. Every other library in the repo has one
  (`ForTauCeti.lean`, `DavisKahan.lean`, `ForMathlib.lean`, the four paper libraries); the
  `[[lean_lib]] name = "Challenge"` block has no root module and no `globs`, so the library
  target can never build and only individual modules ever compile. This is the structural
  reason the conformance gate has been silently vacuous, independent of the stale names
  edward found: nothing ever builds `Challenge` as a whole.
- **Renaming lanes must build `Challenge` explicitly** (jon, 2026-07-27). `defaultTargets`
  is `ForMathlib, ForTauCeti, DavisKahan.All, Acharyya2024, Acharyya2025, DkpsQuench2026,
  Helm2025` — no `Challenge`. A repo-wide grep must also cover `Challenge/` and
  `comparator/*.json`, which restate staged declarations by name. This is exactly how the
  §9.2 regression above got past a green 9266-job build.

- **`Finset.expect` is NOT usable as the canonical mean in our scalar setting**
  (edward, 2026-07-27, probed in Lean). It needs `[Module ℚ≥0 M]`, and for
  `[RCLike 𝕜] [InnerProductSpace 𝕜 E]` that instance does not resolve —
  `NormedSpace ℝ E` is available only through the *def* `InnerProductSpace.rclikeToReal`
  / `NormedSpace.restrictScalars`, deliberately not instances. Relevant to the
  §8.1 `finiteMean` reuse audit.
- **`Finset.centroid 𝕜 s z : E` does typecheck** in that setting (only the
  `noncomputable` complaint), so it *is* a live reuse candidate — but note its
  empty-set behaviour: `Finset.affineCombination` is defined against
  `Classical.arbitrary P`, so `centroid` on `∅` is nonconstructive junk, whereas
  the current `finiteMean` returns `0` there (Mathlib's total-inverse
  convention) and `finiteMean_append` is stated to hold *at* `n = 0`. Any
  swap to `centroid` must re-examine that boundary case.
- **`appendFin z y` is `Fin.snoc z y`** — same `Fin.lastCases` construction, and
  `Fin.snoc_castSucc` / `Fin.snoc_last` are the upstream simp lemmas matching
  `appendFin_castSucc` / `appendFin_last`. The two are *not* `rfl`-equal
  (`snoc` inserts a cast) but are equal by `funext` + `Fin.lastCases` + `simp`.

| **MEASUREMENT — the one escape gating 75 modules is not proof-engineering; it depends on a second escape in another module.** Posted by `jon (yardrat)` 2026-07-30, no lane claimed and no file touched. | `DavisKahan/Experimental/InfiniteDimensional/DoubleAngle.lean:607` and `…/SinTheta/General.lean:632` — read together before taking either. | **The leverage claim is right and the difficulty claim is missing.** The single escape in `DoubleAngle.lean` is `SymmetricNormIdeal.sinAngle_reflected_mem_gauge_eq`: in every symmetric norm ideal, `sinAngleOperator U (reflectedSubspace V U)` and `sinTwoAngleOperator U V` have the same membership and gauge. **Unfolding the two shows why that is not a tactic exercise.** `sinAngleOperator U W = operatorAbsoluteValue (P_U - P_W)` = `CFC.abs (P_U - P_W)`, while `sinTwoAngleOperator U V = 2 • (P_{Uᗮ} ∘L P_V ∘L P_U)`. So the statement is that an *absolute value* and a *triple product* carry the same gauge in every symmetric ideal — a unitary-equivalence theorem, not a rewrite. **The norm-level version is already proved two declarations above** (`doubleAngle_directedGap_identity`: `‖sinTwoAngleOperator U V‖ = directedGap U (reflectedSubspace V U)`) **and it does not generalise**, which is the point: a symmetric gauge sees the whole singular-value sequence, not just its supremum. **The prerequisite is the other escape**, `SymmetricNormIdeal.operatorAbsoluteValue_mem_and_gauge_eq` in `SinTheta/General.lean` — *"symmetric norm ideals contain absolute values with equal gauge (via the polar partial isometry and unitary invariance)"* — which is exactly the tool the `DoubleAngle` escape needs to cross between `CFC.abs T` and `T`. | 2026-07-30 | **not a lane claim — a re-scoping of the existing one.** **Do `operatorAbsoluteValue_mem_and_gauge_eq` first**, and note that the machinery it names now exists where it did not when the row was written: `ForTauCeti/Analysis/InnerProductSpace/PolarPartialIsometry.lean` provides `polarPartial` with `polarPartial_comp_modulus` (the polar identity `M = W \|M\|`), and `Ideals/Symmetric.lean:304` provides `gauge_unitary_conjugation`. **That pair is the stated proof route.** The `DoubleAngle` escape should not be taken before it, or it will be re-derived inline. **Nothing here contradicts the leverage ordering** — `DoubleAngle.lean` is still the right target; it just has one prerequisite, and the row's "1 escape" count hides it because the prerequisite lives in a different file. |

The list: `overlapOp_apply` (`AlignedBasis.lean:123`), `permEV_apply` (`EigenvalueChange.lean:136`), `blockCLM_apply` (`HilbertSchmidtBlock.lean:134`), `columns_apply` and `ofLp_apply` (`HilbertSchmidtLp.lean:75,219`), `intertwiningUnitary_apply` (`IntertwiningUnitary.lean:358`), `opNorm_apply` (`RectangularUnitarilyInvariantNorm/Instances.lean:261`), `norm_sq_cfcHom_apply` (`Rosenblum.lean:202`) and four more — run the measurement in this cell to regenerate it. | **The rubric's words, not an inference:** `TauCetiReview/rubrics/api-design.md` says *"we can avoid making lemmas rely on defeq downstream by using `:= (rfl)` instead of `:= rfl`"*, and `{lane:FTC-EXPOSE-RFL}` already settled the procedure for the `g1`..`g5` conversions — *change `_def`/`_apply` proofs from `rfl` to `(rfl)`*. **That procedure was applied to the converted modules and never swept across the rest.** **Measured 2026-07-30 on the merged tree:** 47 `:= rfl` sites in 27 modules, of which **12** are characteristic lemmas (`_def`/`_apply`/`_iff`/`mem_*`) — those twelve are the lane; the other 35 are ordinary proofs where `rfl` is fine and must not be touched. **Why it matters and why it is cheap:** `:= (rfl)` elaborates the statement without exporting the defeq, so a downstream proof cannot silently depend on the definition unfolding — the same failure `{lane:FTC-EXPOSE-SPECMEAS}` spent four slices unwinding. Each site is a two-character edit; the risk is entirely in what breaks downstream, which is why it needs a build rather than a review. **Needs a build.** **RE-MEASURED 2026-07-31 by `jon (yardrat)` on the merged tree: 29 sites, not 12.** The difference is not new code — it is that a declaration can carry an attribute, and `@[simp] theorem foo_apply … := rfl` does not match a pattern anchored on `theorem` at the start of the line. | **BLOCKED — lane RUB-SHOW. 287 → 4, and the last 4 are held on purpose.** | `show` / `change` tactic steps in `ForTauCeti/**` with no comment in the two preceding lines. **Proof-script edits with no statement change**; take one file at a time. | **The rubric: *`change` and `show` are a code smell: flag any used without a comment documenting why the goal cannot be reached otherwise.*** 290 occurrences have no preceding comment. **The right fix is sometimes not a comment**: the rubric's own preference is *a rewrite to `rfl` or `convert` where one is available*, and many of these are inline `show _ = _ by ring` steps that should be `rw [show … by ring]`-free rewrites or extracted `have`s. The residue — where the goal genuinely cannot be reached otherwise — gets the comment the rubric asks for. **Related but distinct from `RUB-EXPOSE`**: a `show` that only typechecks because a body is exposed is two findings at once, and those are the ones worth doing first. **Needs a build per file.** **RE-MEASURED 2026-07-30: 287 across 74 files** (the row said 290; close enough that the figure was sound, unlike several others on this board). **Slice it by file, heaviest first — the distribution is very uneven and six files hold a quarter of the total:** `Sylvester/Interval.lean` 20, `SinTheta/Perturbation.lean` 18, `RectangularUnitarilyInvariantNorm/Majorization.lean` 13, `LinearPMap/Closed.lean` 12, `SinTheta/DirectedBounds.lean` 12, `LinearPMap/SpectralMeasure.lean` 10. **One file per claim, one build per file** — proof-script edits, so each needs its own compile and there is no cross-file cascade. Re-measure your file's count before and after with the snippet in this row's history rather than trusting the total **ROOT CAUSE FOUND 2026-07-30, and it changes what this lane is.** I probed the heaviest file, `Sylvester/Interval.lean`: **all 20 of its `change` steps are unfolding a definition**, not reformulating a goal — `change N X.adjoint.adjoint = N X` is reaching through `adjointTransport`, `change N (D.toLinearMap ∘ₗ T.toLinearMap) ≤ …` through the local `N'`. **That is the same defect as the `@[expose]` work: a consumer reaching through a definition instead of using its characteristic lemma.** And as there, **the lemma usually already exists** — `adjointTransport_apply` is right there, `@[simp]`. **But it does not fire**, and this is the finding: it is stated as `(adjointTransport N).toFun A.adjoint = N.toFun A`, while every call site uses the *coercion*, `(adjointTransport N) X.adjoint`. Rewriting with it fails *'Did not find an occurrence of the pattern'* — **I tried it on four sites and reverted.** **So the real work is API shape, not proof style:** restate the characteristic lemmas in the coerced form call sites actually use, then the `change` steps become `rw`s. That is why commenting all 287 would satisfy the letter of the rubric and miss the defect entirely **SLICE DONE 2026-07-30: `Sylvester/Interval.lean`, 20 → 0**, and the split is the lesson. **12 eliminated, 8 documented.** The biggest win was **consistency, not cleverness**: four `change` steps unfolding the local `let N'` sat directly beneath two sibling proofs already doing the identical job with `simp only [N', …]`. The file knew the right idiom; those sites had not used it. Six more fell to `simp only [Z]` / `[Y]` for the same reason, and two to the new `adjointTransport_coe_apply`. **The 8 that stayed carry comments saying WHY, not what** — e.g. *'`simp only [Z]` cannot be used here: it normalises the composition further and the following `norm_map` rewrite then has nothing to match'* — which is actionable, unlike *'we use `change` here'*. **Method for the next file, in order: (1) does the definition being unfolded have a characteristic lemma, and is it stated in the COERCED form? (2) does this file already unfold the same thing idiomatically somewhere else — copy that; (3) only then comment, and say what you tried.** Re-measured after: **267 / 73** **SECOND SLICE DONE: `SinTheta/Perturbation.lean`, 18 → 0 — and it is the counter-example to the first.** In `Interval.lean` **12 of 20 were eliminable**; here **0 of 18 were.** I probed before assuming: replaced one `change` with `simp only [BVperp, XUV, AU, CUV]` and the following `rw [← hcomm]` immediately lost its match. **These `change` steps are load-bearing** — they state the goal in the precise syntactic shape the next tactic needs, and `simp only` normalises straight past it. So all 18 took the comment, and **each comment records what was tried**: *'`simp only` on those definitions normalises further and the rewrite then has nothing to match — tried, and it fails here.'* That clause is the point: the next reader will not re-attempt a substitution already disproved. **CORRECTION to this row's earlier claim that the right fix is *usually* not a comment — two files say it is the right fix in one and the wrong fix in the other, and the only way to tell is to try the rewrite and watch it fail.** Expect roughly half of any file to be genuinely load-bearing. Re-measured: **249 / 72** **FIVE FILES DONE, 287 → 216. The running ratio is 12 eliminated to 59 documented, so COMMENTS ARE THE COMMON CASE** — correcting this row twice over, since it first said *usually not a comment* and then *sometimes*. **Each file failed differently, which is why probing beats pattern-matching:** `Interval.lean` 12/20 eliminable via the file's own `simp only [N']` idiom sitting unused two lines away; `Perturbation.lean` 0/18, the `change` steps load-bearing because `simp only` normalises past the shape the next `rw` needs; `Majorization.lean` 0/13, unfolding **private** file-local helpers with no API to route through; `Closed.lean` 0/12, where **`pullback_apply` cannot be written at all** — I tried, and `x : (pullback A e).domain` will not reduce to `pullbackDomain A e` without the body exposed. **That last is structural and worth knowing: the missing `_apply` lemma and the necessary `@[expose]` are two faces of the same fact about `LinearPMap` constructions**, which is why `addBounded`, `perturb`, `unitaryConj` and `specRestrict` are permanent carve-outs on `FTC-EXPOSE-SPECMEAS`. **Every comment records what was attempted**, so the next reader does not retry a disproved substitution **SEVEN FILES DONE, 287 → 194. Ratio 12 eliminated : 81 documented.** **IMPORTANT COUPLING found in `LinearPMap/SpectralMeasure.lean`:** its `change` steps reach through `specProjection` and `spectralPVM`, which carry the deliberate `@[expose]` recorded as debt on **`FTC-EXPOSE-SPECMEAS`**. **There is no lemma to rewrite with until that refactor lands, and whoever does it will remove these `change` steps as a side effect.** Do not spend effort on that file first — it is downstream of the other lane. `DirectedBounds.lean` was another negative probe: `simp only [NU]` normalises past the form `congr 1` closes and leaves unsolved goals in all three parallel developments; 12 documented with that recorded **NINE FILES DONE, 287 → 174. | edward (aiq-gpu) — **lane RUB-SHOW, earlier row — slice: make the characteristic lemmas fire** | `show` / `change` tactic steps in `ForTauCeti/**` with no comment in the two preceding lines. **Proof-script edits with no statement change**; take one file at a time. | **The rubric: *`change` and `show` are a code smell: flag any used without a comment documenting why the goal cannot be reached otherwise.*** 290 occurrences have no preceding comment. **The right fix is usually not a comment**: the rubric's own preference is *a rewrite to `rfl` or `convert` where one is available*, and many of these are inline `show _ = _ by ring` steps that should be `rw [show … by ring]`-free rewrites or extracted `have`s. The residue — where the goal genuinely cannot be reached otherwise — gets the comment the rubric asks for. **Related but distinct from `RUB-EXPOSE`**: a `show` that only typechecks because a body is exposed is two findings at once, and those are the ones worth doing first. **Needs a build per file.** **RE-MEASURED 2026-07-30: 287 across 74 files** (the row said 290; close enough that the figure was sound, unlike several others on this board). **Slice it by file, heaviest first — the distribution is very uneven and six files hold a quarter of the total:** `Sylvester/Interval.lean` 20, `SinTheta/Perturbation.lean` 18, `RectangularUnitarilyInvariantNorm/Majorization.lean` 13, `LinearPMap/Closed.lean` 12, `SinTheta/DirectedBounds.lean` 12, `LinearPMap/SpectralMeasure.lean` 10. **One file per claim, one build per file** — proof-script edits, so each needs its own compile and there is no cross-file cascade. Re-measure your file's count before and after with the snippet in this row's history rather than trusting the total **ROOT CAUSE FOUND 2026-07-30, and it changes what this lane is.** I probed the heaviest file, `Sylvester/Interval.lean`: **all 20 of its `change` steps are unfolding a definition**, not reformulating a goal — `change N X.adjoint.adjoint = N X` is reaching through `adjointTransport`, `change N (D.toLinearMap ∘ₗ T.toLinearMap) ≤ …` through the local `N'`. **That is the same defect as the `@[expose]` work: a consumer reaching through a definition instead of using its characteristic lemma.** And as there, **the lemma usually already exists** — `adjointTransport_apply` is right there, `@[simp]`. **But it does not fire**, and this is the finding: it is stated as `(adjointTransport N).toFun A.adjoint = N.toFun A`, while every call site uses the *coercion*, `(adjointTransport N) X.adjoint`. Rewriting with it fails *'Did not find an occurrence of the pattern'* — **I tried it on four sites and reverted.** **So the real work is API shape, not proof style:** restate the characteristic lemmas in the coerced form call sites actually use, then the `change` steps become `rw`s. That is why commenting all 287 would satisfy the letter of the rubric and miss the defect entirely | 2026-07-30 | claimed 2026-07-30, verified free and row pushed before the first edit. `superseded — the completion row carries the result; marker dropped on merge by `edward (aiq-gpu-docs)` per the board's own rule` **Taking the root cause, not a file:** restate `adjointTransport_apply` in the coerced form so it fires at call sites. That is upstream of the `change` steps in `Sylvester/Interval.lean` and anywhere else reaching through the same definition — fixing the lemma is worth more than rewriting the consumers one at a time |

The rest — `BlockLowerBound`, `ProjValMeasure/Additivity`, `LinearPMap/{GraphCore,RealLowerBound,SpectralCutOperator,SpectralFormBounds,SpectralGrid,SpectralProjectionGroup}`, `Sylvester/Internal/SpectralBounds` — are this project's own shapes, and their docstrings already say what makes a naive version insufficient; **they get an explicit *follows nothing in particular* line, which is the honest answer and stops the next audit re-reporting them.** I will cite by author and result, and by in-tree `prose/` file where one exists, **not by page or theorem number** — a citation I cannot verify from this tree is worse than none. | 2026-07-30 | **claimed** **UPDATE — DONE.** All 32 modules now carry a `## Sources` block, and the split came out **23 cited, 9 explicitly following nothing**. That is equivalent here and strictly simpler to work with — `Φ` is permutation-invariant (`symm`) and monotone (`mono`), so the supremum is already rearrangement-independent, and the decreasing rearrangement is a device for *computing* the value, not part of its specification.
